# AGENT.md

This file is the live memory of the Vantrilex Arsenal project. It is seeded
from the repository state observed on 2026-10-13 and updated additively;
every task reads it first and updates it last.

## Identity

Vantrilex Arsenal is an OpenCode plugin plus a component Registry, living at
`O:\vantrilex-arsenal`. It provisions a curated kit of agent components onto
a target project and governs how that kit is used. It serves the solo
developer who equips projects through the three top-level skills
(`vantrilex-prime`, `vantrilex-vanguard`, `vantrilex-doctrine`) plus the
workflow forge. Success is the kit staying honest: the verifiers exit 0,
every locked component resolves to a definition and a registry entry, and
the docs describe the tree as it is.

- 2026-10-21 — **OWNER RULING — RELEASE SYSTEM (recorded VERBATIM here and in WORKFLOW.md; supersedes the human-first Amendment #1 reading; amends §7 for auto-published lines; named releases 0.8.7/0.9 mechanics stay as ruled; runs as a PARALLEL workstream — the cluster sequence continues untouched; single-writer on the state files):**
  **§1 NUMBERING LAW (standing):** a completed PHASE publishes as +0.0.1; a completed MISSION publishes as +0.1.0 (minor +1, patch reset). Unit names are the project's own records (MISSION-PLAN = mission; Phase 0/1 = phases; slices/clusters per the records — borderline calls flagged in the map, §3, not decided silently).
  **§2 SELF-PUBLISH MECHANISM:** extend auto-publish with a self-publish path — queue file `.opencode/release-queue.jsonl` (entry per closed unit: unit type, scope summary = release notes, base commit); on push to main with a pending entry: compute the next number by §1 from the highest existing tag; VERIFY BEFORE CREATE (full battery at the exact commit; red ⇒ create nothing, log + report); green ⇒ create tag + release (gh, workflow token) + deterministic tarball + audit line + mark entry published. Idempotent, single-flight. Kill switch `.opencode/auto-release.enabled` gates the self-publish path only.
  **§3 BACKFILL MAP FIRST (HARD GATE):** reconstruct the version map from 0.7.7 to the latest completed unit, from evidence only (existing tags, commit history, closure records: factory P0–P5 + audit + remediation, v87 plan nodes, POST87 Phase 0 + slice 1 + waves, cluster gates as they close). Deliverable: a table (unit → commit → computed version → as-closed evidence). NO backfill publishes before the owner approves the map.
  **§4 BACKFILL PUBLICATION (after map approval):** publish in ascending version order, each at its own commit, tree as it was; release notes carry the scope + the AS-CLOSED verification evidence, with any evidence gap stated plainly. Never edit a historical tree to pass today's battery; never let today's battery block a historical release. Each publish is audit-logged; progress reported per version.
  **§5 GOING FORWARD:** every future phase/mission closure appends its queue entry at closure; the line self-publishes by §1–§2 under the verify-before-create gate (fail-closed). The first live self-publish (backfill's first or forward's first, whichever fires first) runs MONITORED, and its audit line closes the §2.3 end-to-end debt.
  **§6 PROOFS for the mechanism (dry-run, reported before the first live publish):** pending entry + green tree ⇒ WOULD publish (number/notes/asset shown); red tree ⇒ refuses; existing tag ⇒ skips; switch absent ⇒ no self-publish.
  **§7 Fences unchanged:** state files uncommitted; inherited tree inventory-only; Q-15 twin held; no force-push, no history rewrite anywhere in this workstream.
  **§8 INCREMENT LAW — ARSENAL DEFAULT, USER-CONFIGURABLE (standing doctrine):** §8.1 the increment principle is a LAW of the release system, shipped IN the kit as the DEFAULT versioning policy: completed phase = +0.0.1 · completed mission = +0.1.0 (standard cascade: a higher increment resets the lower components). §8.2 it is a DEFAULT, not a lock: the increments live in ONE user-editable policy file inside the kit — the single source the publisher reads at release time. No increment value is hard-coded in any script or workflow. Any user may restyle the increments in that file, and the publisher applies whatever the policy says. §8.3 this repo runs the shipped defaults unchanged; the release system's documentation states the policy (what counts as a phase vs a mission, the default increments, and how a user changes them).
  **WORKSTREAM:** `rel-map` (evidence-only backfill map 0.7.7 → latest closed unit; hard gate) ∥ `rel-selfpub` (mechanism: queue + policy file + kill switch + verify-before-create + idempotent/single-flight + §6 dry-run proofs). No backfill publish before map approval; no live publish now; doc section follows S5.

## Decisions

- Single-writer law: each `registry/data/*.jsonl` sidecar has exactly one
  owning branch at a time. Sourced from `AGENTS.md`; decider unverified —
  no decision record names one.
- No invented install commands: a component's `install_cmd` is verified or
  explicitly `null` with `verification: unverified`. Sourced from
  `AGENTS.md`; decider unverified — no decision record names one.
- New `cli` entries default to `tier conditional`, recorded as prose
  rather than a schema mechanism. Sourced from `CHANGELOG.md` 0.3.0;
  decider unverified — the changelog entry names no decider.
- The six non-migrated hooks stay on the V1 surface and are inert under
  V2; porting them is a separate concern, deliberately not bundled.
  Sourced from `docs/16-TASK-DISPATCH.md`; decider unverified — the doc
  names no decider.
- This lane owns only `AGENT.md` (new), `WORKFLOW.md` (new), and one
  appended pointer section in `AGENTS.md`, with no commit. Sourced from
  the 2026-10-13 NODE DOG work order; decider: the dispatching session.
- NODE T3 single-home ruling 2026-10-14: each merged item lives in exactly one root file — standing directives in `AGENTS.md`, orchestration/verification/runtime method in `WORKFLOW.md` Part B, lane records here; the other files point, never restate. Sourced from the NODE T3 work order; decider: the dispatching session.
- NODE T3 lane discipline 2026-10-14: this lane writes only the three owned files, makes no commit, and uses free models only. Observance is recorded here; the standing rules live in `AGENTS.md` and carry no copy here. Sourced from the NODE T3 work order; decider: the dispatching session.
- Orchestrator-only write 2026-10-14: only the orchestrator writes this file, after every task, rotating closed entries into `docs/agent-log-YYYY-MM.md`. Sourced from doctrine Ritual 2; decider unverified — the doctrine states the role and no decision record names one.

## Known risks & defects

- Concurrent sibling writes (CK lane on lock/catalog) while this lane
  writes root memory files. Evidence: work order text 2026-10-13.
  Mitigation observed: disjoint file sets plus the single-writer law; risk
  is sequencing, not overlap.
- Six locked hooks plus `docsDisciplineGuard` are inert under OpenCode V2
  (only `task-dispatcher` runs). Evidence: `docs/16-TASK-DISPATCH.md`
  2026-10-13 read. Deliberate and documented, not a silent failure.
- Unverified `install_cmd` entries exist by design; a plausible-looking
  wrong command would be worse. Evidence: `AGENTS.md` hard rule 2.
- Markdownlint runner availability was unverified — no markdownlint binary
  in `node_modules/.bin` at seeding time; the verification step must
  report its actual runner and exit code, and a check that cannot run is a
  failed check.
- Pointer drift between the root files and the canonical skill text (doctrine B.8.2, Vanguard A.7d). Evidence: pointers cite sections, never copy text (NODE T3 drafts 2026-10-14). Mitigation observed: single-home census at every docs gate; a census failure returns for re-homing before any other gate runs.
